# Opinion polling for the 2026 Israeli legislative election

::: {#o1 .subject cue=lead name="Opinion polling for the 2026 Israeli legislative election"}

In the run up to the [2026 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Israeli_legislative_election), various organisations are conducting [opinion polling](wikipedia://en/277315) to gauge voting intention in [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel) during the term of the [twenty-fifth Knesset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_members_of_the_twenty-fifth_Knesset). This article lists the results of such polls. The date range for these opinion polls is from the [2022 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Israeli_legislative_election), held on 1 November, to the present day. In keeping with the [election silence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election_silence) law, no polls may be published from the end of the Friday before the election until the [polling stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polling_station) close on election day at 22:00.[^o2]

:::

Polls are listed in reverse chronological order and using the dates when the survey fieldwork was done, as opposed to the date of publication. Where the fieldwork dates are unknown, the date of publication is given instead. The highest figure in each polling survey is displayed in bold with its background shaded in the leading party's colour. If a tie occurs, this is applied to the highest figures.

Shlomo Filber is affiliated with Likud and its leader [Benjamin Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Netanyahu). Filber is implicated in [Case 4000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Case_4000) on charges of breach of trust; thus it may be advisable to approach opinion polls edited by him with caution.[^o37]

## Seat projections {#Seat_projections}

This section displays voting intention estimates referring to the next [Knesset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knesset) election. The figures listed are Knesset seat counts rather than percentages, unless otherwise stated.

### Graphical summary {#Graphical_summary}

This graph shows the polling trends from the [2022 Israeli legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Israeli_legislative_election) until the next election day using [local regressions](wikipedia://en/4146592) (LOESS). Scenario polls are not included here. For parties not crossing the [electoral threshold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_threshold) (3.25%) in any given poll, the number of seats is calculated as a percentage of the 120 total seats.

Opinion polls Israel 2026.svg Interactive poll charts

### Guide to the table {#Guide_to_the_table}

The parties which hold Knesset seats are listed in descending order by the number of seats they received in the 2022 election: [Likud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud), [Together](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Together_%28Israel%29) ([Yesh Atid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yesh_Atid) competed on its own previously but will run with [Bennett 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bennett_2026) in this election), the [Religious Zionist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_Zionist_Party) (which formed a [technical bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technical_bloc) with [Zehut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zehut) this election) and [Otzma Yehudit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otzma_Yehudit) (which previously ran with RZP), [Blue and White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_and_White_%28political_party%29), [Shas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shas), [United Torah Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Torah_Judaism) (which consists of [Agudat Yisrael](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agudat_Yisrael) and [Degel HaTorah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Degel_HaTorah)), [Yisrael Beiteinu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yisrael_Beiteinu), [Ra'am](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ra'am) (which formed a technical bloc with [Yoav Segalovitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Segalovitz) this election), the [Joint List](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_List) (which previously ran separately as [Hadash–Ta'al](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hadash–Ta'al) and [Balad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balad_%28political_party%29)), followed by [The Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Democrats_%28Israel%29) (which won seats as [Labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Labor_Party) and merged with [Meretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meretz) to form the new party).

The table also includes new parties that do not hold seats, specifically [Yashar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yashar_%28political_party%29), [The Reservists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Reservists_%28political_party%29), [Amcha Yisrael](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amcha_Yisrael), and [Haredi Public](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haredi_Public). Parties that are not polled on consistently are listed in footnotes, including [Noam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noam_%28political_party%29), which currently holds a seat.

Parties that fall below the [electoral threshold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_threshold) of 3.25% are normally denoted by the percentage of votes that they received, shown in parentheses, e.g. (1.2%), rather than by the number of seats they would have received. If the publisher of a poll chooses to provide a figure for the number of seats a party would receive if the electoral threshold did not exist, this figure is shown in parentheses *without* a percentage sign, e.g. (1). If a poll has no information on a particular party, or states that it does not meet the electoral threshold without providing details, that party is instead marked by a dash.

The column headed Others contains figures for assorted smaller parties in cases where publishers provide these numbers. A slightly different convention is used here. If the publisher provides percentages for these parties, then the sum of these figures is shown, without parentheses. If the publisher provides projected seat numbers (disregarding the electoral threshold) then the sum of these figures is shown, in parentheses. In both cases, the breakdown by party is given in a footnote.

The party receiving the highest number of projected seats has its figure displayed in bold with the background shaded in the party's colour. In the case of a tie, the parties tying for the highest number of projected seats have their figures displayed in bold with the backgrounds shaded in their colours.

The [37th government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirty-seventh_government_of_Israel) parties and alliances (Likud, Religious Zionist Party, Otzma Yehudit, Shas, and United Torah Judaism) are highlighted. The sum of their projected seats is shown in the column headed "Gov." If that sum constitutes a majority of the Knesset (61 or more seats), it is highlighted and bolded.

## Voting intention polls (reported as percentages) {#Voting_intention_polls_(reported_as_percentages)}

The following polls reported raw percentages of responses without calculating seat projections, with voting share only for respondents who expressed a preference for a particular party option. In cases where respondents indicated that they will not vote, or declined to respond, the percentages were recalculated to omit them.

## Scenario polls {#Scenario_polls}

Most often, opinion polling about hypothetical scenarios is done in the same survey as the regular polling. This is why these scenario polls are paired for comparison purposes.

#### Likud B, Benjamin Netanyahu, Ofer Winter parties and Reservists-B&W merger {#Likud_B,_Benjamin_Netanyahu,_Ofer_Winter_parties_and_Reservists-B&W_merger}

New parties from [Benjamin Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Netanyahu), [Ofer Winter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ofer_Winter), and a right-wing bloc ("Likud B") between combinations of [Aliza Bloch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliza_Bloch), [Yuli Edelstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuli_Edelstein), [Gilad Erdan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilad_Erdan), [Sharren Haskel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharren_Haskel), [Moshe Kahlon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Kahlon), and [Ayelet Shaked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayelet_Shaked).

## Other questions {#Other_questions}

### Whether voters intend to vote for the same party that they did in 2022 {#Whether_voters_intend_to_vote_for_the_same_party_that_they_did_in_2022}

The [Israel Democracy Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Democracy_Institute) commissioned the Viterbi Center to gauge voting intentions of Israeli voters based on their voting history. In January 2023, they asked if voters would vote for the same party as they did in 2022. In that poll, 50% were certain they would, 22% think yes, 7% think no, and 4% were certain that they would not. An additional 3% of those polled did not vote in 2022 but planned to in the next election, 6% did not and do not plan to vote, and 10% were undecided.[^o500]

In October 2023, the question was asked again but centered around voting block.[^o501] In that poll, 39.7% would vote for the same party as was voted for in 2022, 14.8% would vote for the same political bloc, 6.3% would vote for a party in a different bloc, and a combined 39.2% of those surveyed either wouldn't vote or were undecided. The Israel Democracy Institute also ran a series of surveys in 2024 asking people their re-voting intention, based on which party they voted for.[^o335]

"עכשיו קצת בעולמות הדמיון - מה קורה אם הלא כשירכן, גם ניר ברקת, עד לא מזמן היורש הטבעי, מביא מנדטים. פחות מיוסי כהן. מה שאומר: יוסי כהן הוא איום רציני מאוד על כל מי שרואה עצמו יורש בליכוד. חוזר: יוסי כהן עף גבוה בליכוד, אם יחליט להצטרף למפלגה הפרובלמטית הזו. מר ברקת, השקעת לא מעט בלהיות שקוף..."
